### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements for catalytic properties of transition metals.
Statement I: First row transition metals which act as catalyst utilise their 3d electrons only for formation of bonds between reactant molecules and atoms on the surface of catalyst.
Statement II: There is increase in the concentration of reactants on the surface of catalyst which strengthens the bonds in reacting molecules.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I is incorrect because first row transition metals utilise both 3d and 4s electrons for the formation of bonds between reactant molecules and atoms on the surface of the catalyst.

Statement II is incorrect because the increase in the concentration of reactants on the surface of the catalyst weakens the bonds in the reacting molecules, which lowers the activation energy.

Therefore, both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: The number of pairs among $[\text{Ti}^{4+}, \text{V}^{2+}]$, $[\text{V}^{2+}, \text{Mn}^{2+}]$, $[\text{Mn}^{2+}, \text{Fe}^{3+}]$ and $[\text{V}^{2+}, \text{Cr}^{2+}]$ in which both ions are coloured is $3$.
Statement II: The number of pairs among $[\text{La}^{3+}, \text{Yb}^{2+}]$, $[\text{Lu}^{3+}, \text{Ce}^{4+}]$ and $[\text{Ac}^{3+}, \text{Lr}^{3+}]$ ions in which both are diamagnetic is $3$.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

For Statement I:
The electronic configurations of the given transition metal ions are:
$\text{Ti}^{4+} : 3d^0$ (colourless due to absence of unpaired electrons)
$\text{V}^{2+} : 3d^3$ (coloured)
$\text{Mn}^{2+} : 3d^5$ (coloured)
$\text{Fe}^{3+} : 3d^5$ (coloured)
$\text{Cr}^{2+} : 3d^4$ (coloured)

The pairs in which both ions are coloured are $[\text{V}^{2+}, \text{Mn}^{2+}]$, $[\text{Mn}^{2+}, \text{Fe}^{3+}]$, and $[\text{V}^{2+}, \text{Cr}^{2+}]$. The pair $[\text{Ti}^{4+}, \text{V}^{2+}]$ contains $\text{Ti}^{4+}$ which is colourless. Thus, there are exactly $3$ pairs where both ions are coloured. Statement I is correct.

For Statement II:
The electronic configurations of the given f-block ions are:
$\text{La}^{3+} : [\text{Xe}] 4f^0$ (diamagnetic)
$\text{Yb}^{2+} : [\text{Xe}] 4f^{14}$ (diamagnetic)
$\text{Lu}^{3+} : [\text{Xe}] 4f^{14}$ (diamagnetic)
$\text{Ce}^{4+} : [\text{Xe}] 4f^0$ (diamagnetic)
$\text{Ac}^{3+} : [\text{Rn}] 5f^0$ (diamagnetic)
$\text{Lr}^{3+} : [\text{Rn}] 5f^{14}$ (diamagnetic)

All the given ions have either an $f^0$ or $f^{14}$ configuration, meaning they have no unpaired electrons and are diamagnetic. Therefore, in all $3$ pairs, both ions are diamagnetic. Statement II is correct.

Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are correct

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of first $(\Delta_i H_1)$ and second $(\Delta_i H_2)$ ionisation enthalpy values of Cr and Mn are :
A. $\Delta_i H_1 :$ Cr $>$ Mn
B. $\Delta_i H_2 :$ Cr $>$ Mn
C. $\Delta_i H_1 :$ Mn $>$ Cr
D. $\Delta_i H_2 :$ Mn $>$ Cr
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B only
- **(B)** B and C only
- **(C)** A and D only
- **(D)** C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) B and C only**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electronic configuration of Cr is $[Ar] 3d^5 4s^1$ and Mn is $[Ar] 3d^5 4s^2$.

For the first ionization enthalpy, the electron is removed from the $4s$ orbital. Mn has a fully filled $4s^2$ configuration and a higher effective nuclear charge compared to Cr. Thus, the first ionization enthalpy of Mn is greater than that of Cr ($\Delta_i H_1 :$ Mn $>$ Cr). This makes statement C correct.

After the first ionization, the electronic configurations become:
$Cr^+ : [Ar] 3d^5$
$Mn^+ : [Ar] 3d^5 4s^1$

For the second ionization enthalpy, the electron is removed from the highly stable half-filled $3d^5$ subshell in $Cr^+$, whereas in $Mn^+$, it is removed from the $4s^1$ orbital. Removing an electron from a stable half-filled configuration requires significantly more energy. Thus, the second ionization enthalpy of Cr is greater than that of Mn ($\Delta_i H_2 :$ Cr $>$ Mn). This makes statement B correct.

Therefore, the correct statements are B and C.

Answer: B and C only

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The correct statements among the following are.
A. Basic vanadium oxide is used in the manufacture of $\text{H}_2\text{SO}_4$.
B. The spin-only magnetic moment value of the transition metal halide employed in Ziegler-Natta polymerization is $2.84$ BM.
C. The p-block metal compound employed in Ziegler-Natta polymerization has the metal in $+3$ oxidation state.
D. The number of electrons present in the outer most 'd' orbital of metal halide employed in Wacker process is $8$.

Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B Only
- **(B)** A, C and D Only
- **(C)** C and D Only
- **(D)** B, C and D Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) C and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement A: Vanadium pentoxide ($\text{V}_2\text{O}_5$) is used as a catalyst in the manufacture of $\text{H}_2\text{SO}_4$ by the Contact process. $\text{V}_2\text{O}_5$ is an amphoteric oxide, not a basic oxide. Thus, statement A is incorrect.

Statement B: The transition metal halide used in Ziegler-Natta polymerization is $\text{TiCl}_4$. In $\text{TiCl}_4$, titanium is in the $+4$ oxidation state with a $3\text{d}^0$ electronic configuration. The number of unpaired electrons is zero, so its spin-only magnetic moment is $0$ BM. Thus, statement B is incorrect.

Statement C: The p-block metal compound used in Ziegler-Natta polymerization is triethylaluminium, $\text{Al}(\text{C}_2\text{H}_5)_3$. Aluminium is a p-block element and is in the $+3$ oxidation state. Thus, statement C is correct.

Statement D: The metal halide employed in the Wacker process is $\text{PdCl}_2$. Palladium (Pd) has the electronic configuration $[\text{Kr}] 4\text{d}^{10}$. In $\text{PdCl}_2$, Pd is in the $+2$ oxidation state, giving it a $4\text{d}^8$ configuration. The number of electrons in the outermost d-orbital is $8$. Thus, statement D is correct.

Therefore, only statements C and D are correct.

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
Statement I: Presence of large number of unpaired electrons in transition metal atoms results in higher enthalpies of their atomisation.
Statement II: $d_{xy} = d_{xz} = d_{yz} < d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2}$ and $d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2} < d_{xy} = d_{xz} = d_{yz}$ are the d-orbital splittings in $[Fe(H_2O)_6]^{3+}$ and $[Ni(Cl)_4]^{2-}$ complex ions respectively.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: The enthalpy of atomisation of transition metals depends on the strength of metallic bonding. A greater number of unpaired electrons in the d-orbitals leads to stronger metallic bonding, which results in a higher enthalpy of atomisation. Thus, Statement I is correct.

Statement II: $[Fe(H_2O)_6]^{3+}$ is an octahedral complex. In an octahedral crystal field, the d-orbitals split such that the $t_{2g}$ set of orbitals ($d_{xy}, d_{xz}, d_{yz}$) has lower energy than the $e_g$ set of orbitals ($d_{x^2-y^2}, d_{z^2}$). Therefore, $d_{xy} = d_{xz} = d_{yz} < d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2}$.

$[Ni(Cl)_4]^{2-}$ is a tetrahedral complex because $Cl^{-}$ is a weak field ligand and the coordination number is $4$. In a tetrahedral crystal field, the splitting is reversed compared to the octahedral field. The $e$ set of orbitals ($d_{x^2-y^2}, d_{z^2}$) has lower energy than the $t_2$ set of orbitals ($d_{xy}, d_{xz}, d_{yz}$). Therefore, $d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2} < d_{xy} = d_{xz} = d_{yz}$. Thus, Statement II is also correct.

Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are correct

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following is NOT a physical or chemical characteristics of interstitial compounds?

**Options:**

- **(A)** They have high melting points, higher than those of pure metals.
- **(B)** They are very soft and ionic in nature.
- **(C)** They retain metallic conductivity.
- **(D)** They are chemically inert and usually non-stoichiometric.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) They are very soft and ionic in nature.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Interstitial compounds are formed when small atoms like H, C, or N are trapped inside the crystal lattices of transition metals.

The principal physical and chemical characteristics of interstitial compounds are:
(i) They have high melting points, higher than those of pure metals.
(ii) They are very hard, and some borides approach diamond in hardness.
(iii) They retain metallic conductivity.
(iv) They are chemically inert.
(v) They are usually non-stoichiometric.

Therefore, the statement that they are very soft and ionic in nature is incorrect. They are very hard and are neither typically ionic nor covalent.

Answer: They are very soft and ionic in nature.

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider $|x|$ is the difference in oxidation states of Mn in highest manganese fluoride and highest manganese oxide. The ions with $|x|$ number of unpaired electrons from the following are:
A. $Sc^{3+}$
B. $Zn^{2+}$
C. $V^{2+}$
D. $Fe^{2+}$
E. $Co^{2+}$
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B Only
- **(B)** C, D and E Only
- **(C)** C and E Only
- **(D)** B and E Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) C and E Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

The highest manganese fluoride is $MnF_4$, where the oxidation state of Mn is $+4$.

The highest manganese oxide is $Mn_2O_7$, where the oxidation state of Mn is $+7$.

The difference in oxidation states is $|x| = |7 - 4| = 3$.

The number of unpaired electrons in the given ions are:
$Sc^{3+}$: $[Ar] 3d^0 \Rightarrow 0$ unpaired electrons
$Zn^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^{10} \Rightarrow 0$ unpaired electrons
$V^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^3 \Rightarrow 3$ unpaired electrons
$Fe^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^6 \Rightarrow 4$ unpaired electrons
$Co^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^7 \Rightarrow 3$ unpaired electrons

Thus, $V^{2+}$ (C) and $Co^{2+}$ (E) have $3$ unpaired electrons.

Answer: C and E Only

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The correct statements among the following are,
A. $Mo(VI)$ and $W(VI)$ are less stable than $Cr(VI)$.
B. $Ce^{4+}$ and $Tb^{4+}$ are oxidant while $Eu^{2+}$ and $Yb^{2+}$ are reductant.
C. $Cm$ and $Am$ have seven unpaired electrons.
D. Actinoid contraction is greater from element to element than lanthanoid contraction.

Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B Only
- **(B)** C and D Only
- **(C)** B and D Only
- **(D)** A and C Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) B and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

In d-block elements, the stability of higher oxidation states increases down a group. Therefore, $Mo(VI)$ and $W(VI)$ are more stable than $Cr(VI)$. Statement A is incorrect.

The most stable oxidation state for lanthanoids is $+3$. Ions like $Ce^{4+}$ and $Tb^{4+}$ tend to gain an electron to achieve the $+3$ state, acting as oxidants. Conversely, $Eu^{2+}$ and $Yb^{2+}$ tend to lose an electron to achieve the $+3$ state, acting as reductants. Statement B is correct.

The electronic configuration of $Am$ is $[Rn] 5f^{7} 7s^{2}$, which has $7$ unpaired electrons. The electronic configuration of $Cm$ is $[Rn] 5f^{7} 6d^{1} 7s^{2}$, which has $8$ unpaired electrons ($7$ in $5f$ and $1$ in $6d$). Statement C is incorrect.

The $5f$ electrons have a poorer shielding effect compared to $4f$ electrons. As a result, the effective nuclear charge increases more significantly across the actinoid series, leading to a greater actinoid contraction from element to element than the lanthanoid contraction. Statement D is correct.

Thus, only statements B and D are correct.

Answer: B and D Only

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Number of paramagnetic ions among the following d- and f-block metal ions is _______.
$Mn^{2+}, Cu^{2+}, Zn^{2+}, Yb^{2+}, Sc^{3+}, La^{3+}, Gd^{3+}, Lu^{3+}, Ti^{4+}, Ce^{4+}$
(Atomic number of $Mn = 25, Cu = 29, Zn = 30, Yb = 70, Sc = 21, La = 57, Gd = 64, Lu = 71, Ti = 22, Ce = 58$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

The magnetic nature of an ion depends on the presence of unpaired electrons. Ions with at least one unpaired electron are paramagnetic, while those with all paired electrons are diamagnetic.

Let us write the electronic configurations of the given ions:

$Mn^{2+} (Z=25) : [Ar] 3d^5$ $\rightarrow$ $5$ unpaired electrons (Paramagnetic)
$Cu^{2+} (Z=29) : [Ar] 3d^9$ $\rightarrow$ $1$ unpaired electron (Paramagnetic)
$Zn^{2+} (Z=30) : [Ar] 3d^{10}$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)
$Yb^{2+} (Z=70) : [Xe] 4f^{14}$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)
$Sc^{3+} (Z=21) : [Ar] 3d^0$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)
$La^{3+} (Z=57) : [Xe] 4f^0$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)
$Gd^{3+} (Z=64) : [Xe] 4f^7$ $\rightarrow$ $7$ unpaired electrons (Paramagnetic)
$Lu^{3+} (Z=71) : [Xe] 4f^{14}$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)
$Ti^{4+} (Z=22) : [Ar] 3d^0$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)
$Ce^{4+} (Z=58) : [Xe] 4f^0$ $\rightarrow$ $0$ unpaired electrons (Diamagnetic)

The paramagnetic ions are $Mn^{2+}$, $Cu^{2+}$, and $Gd^{3+}$.

Total number of paramagnetic ions = $3$.

Answer: $3$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following statements about manganate and permanganate ions. Identify the correct statements. 
 A. The geometry of both manganate and permanganate ions is tetrahedral. 
 B. The oxidation states of Mn in manganate and permanganate are +7 and +6, respectively. 
 C. Oxidation of Mn (II) salt by peroxodisulphate gives manganate ion as the final product. 
 D. Manganate ion is paramagnetic and permanganate ions is diamagnetic. 
 E. Acidified permanganate ion reduces oxalate, nitrite and iodide ions. 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, C and D Only
- **(B)** A and D Only
- **(C)** A, D and E Only
- **(D)** A, B and C Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Manganate ion → $\text{MnO}_4^{2-}$
Permanganate ion → $\text{MnO}_4^{-}$
(A) Both are tetrahedral ($d^3s$ hybridisation) TRUE
(B) $\text{MnO}_4^{-}$ has Mn in +7 oxidation state; $\text{MnO}_4^{2-}$ has Mn in +6 oxidation state. The statement says +7 for manganate and +6 for permanganate, which is reversed. FALSE
(C) Oxidation of $\text{Mn}^{2+}$ by peroxodisulphate ($\text{S}_2\text{O}_8^{2-}$) gives permanganate ion ($\text{MnO}_4^{-}$), not manganate ion. FALSE
(D) $\text{MnO}_4^{-}$ is diamagnetic; $\text{MnO}_4^{2-}$ is paramagnetic. TRUE
(E) Acidified permanganate ion oxidises (not reduces) oxalate, nitrite and iodide ions. FALSE
Correct statements: A and D only.

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The number of pairs, from the following, in which both the ions are coloured in aqueous solution is 3. $\left[\mathrm{Sc}^{3+}, \mathrm{Ti}^{3+}\right],\left[\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}, \mathrm{Cr}^{2+}\right],\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}, \mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\right]$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}, \mathrm{Ti}^{4+}\right]$ 
 <b>Statement II</b>: $\mathrm{Th}^{4+}$ is the strongest reducing agent among $\mathrm{Th}^{4+}, \mathrm{Ce}^{4+}, \mathrm{Gd}^{3+}$ and $\mathrm{Eu}^{2+}$. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are false**

**Detailed Solution:**

[Sc³⁺, Ti³⁺] has Sc³⁺ colorless (no d-electrons) and Ti³⁺ colored;
[Mn²⁺, Cr²⁺] has Mn²⁺ colorless (d⁵ half-filled, no d-d transitions) and Cr²⁺ colored;
[Cu²⁺, Zn²⁺] has Cu²⁺ colored and Zn²⁺ colorless (d¹⁰ filled);
[Ni²⁺, Ti⁴⁺] has Ni²⁺ colored and Ti⁴⁺ colorless (no d-electrons).
Zero pairs have both ions colored. Statement I is FALSE.

Th⁴⁺ is not a reducing agent. Statement II is FALSE.

Both statements are false.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Among the following oxides of 3 d elements, the number of mixed oxides are $\_\_\_\_$。 $\mathrm{Ti}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3}, \mathrm{~V}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{4}, \mathrm{Cr}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3}, \mathrm{Mn}_{3} \mathrm{O}_{4}, \mathrm{Fe}_{3} \mathrm{O}_{4}, \mathrm{Fe}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3}, \mathrm{Co}_{3} \mathrm{O}_{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mixed oxides contain the same element in two different oxidation states.
Analyzing each oxide:
Ti₂O₃ contains Ti(+3) only - simple oxide.
V₂O₄ contains V(+4) only - simple oxide.
Cr₂O₃ contains Cr(+3) only - simple oxide.
Mn₃O₄ can be written as MnO·Mn₂O₃, containing both Mn(+2) and Mn(+3) - mixed oxide.
Fe₃O₄ can be written as FeO·Fe₂O₃, containing both Fe(+2) and Fe(+3) - mixed oxide.
Fe₂O₃ contains Fe(+3) only - simple oxide.
Co₃O₄ can be written as CoO·Co₂O₃, containing both Co(+2) and Co(+3) - mixed oxide.
Therefore, three mixed oxides are present: Mn₃O₄, Fe₃O₄, and Co₃O₄.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements : 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The first ionization enthalpy of Cr is lower than that of Mn . 
 <b>Statement II</b>: The second and third ionization enthalpies of Cr are higher than those of Mn. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I:
The electronic configuration of $Cr$ is $[Ar] 3d^5 4s^1$ and $Mn$ is $[Ar] 3d^5 4s^2$.
For $Cr$, the first electron is removed from $4s^1$, while for $Mn$, it is removed from a stable fully filled $4s^2$ subshell.
Additionally, $Mn$ has a higher nuclear charge.
Thus, the first ionization enthalpy ($IE_1$) of $Cr$ ($653 \text{ kJ/mol}$) is lower than that of $Mn$ ($717 \text{ kJ/mol}$).
Statement I is true.

Statement II:
For the second ionization enthalpy ($IE_2$), $Cr^+$ has configuration $[Ar] 3d^5$ (stable half-filled) and $Mn^+$ has $[Ar] 3d^5 4s^1$.
Removing an electron from the stable $3d^5$ configuration of $Cr^+$ requires more energy than removing it from the $4s^1$ of $Mn^+$.
Thus, $IE_2$ of $Cr$ ($1592 \text{ kJ/mol}$) is higher than $Mn$ ($1509 \text{ kJ/mol}$).
For the third ionization enthalpy ($IE_3$), $Cr^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^4$ and $Mn^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^5$.
Removing an electron from the stable half-filled $3d^5$ configuration of $Mn^{2+}$ requires significantly more energy than from the $3d^4$ of $Cr^{2+}$.
Thus, $IE_3$ of $Mn$ ($3248 \text{ kJ/mol}$) is higher than $Cr$ ($2987 \text{ kJ/mol}$).
Since $IE_3$ of $Cr$ is lower than $Mn$, Statement II is false.

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A first row transition metal $(\mathrm{M})$ does not liberate $\mathrm{H}_{2}$ gas from dilute HCl.1 mol of aqueous solution of $\mathrm{MSO}_{4}$ is treated with excess of aqueous KCN and then $\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{~S}(\mathrm{~g})$ is passed through the solution. The amount of MS (metal sulphide) formed from the above reaction is $\_\_\_\_$ mol.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** 1
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0**

**Detailed Solution:**

The first row transition metal (M) that does not liberate $H_2$ gas from dilute $HCl$ is Copper ($Cu$), as it has a positive reduction potential ($E^\circ_{Cu^{2+}/Cu} = +0.34\text{ V}$).
When $1\text{ mol}$ of $CuSO_4$ reacts with excess aqueous $KCN$, it first forms $Cu(CN)_2$, which is unstable and decomposes to $CuCN$ and cyanogen gas $(CN)_2$.
$2CuSO_4 + 4KCN \rightarrow 2Cu(CN)_2 + 2K_2SO_4$
$2Cu(CN)_2 \rightarrow 2CuCN + (CN)_2$
The $CuCN$ then dissolves in excess $KCN$ to form a highly stable soluble complex, potassium tetracyanocuprate(I):
$CuCN + 3KCN \rightarrow K_3[Cu(CN)_4]$
The complex $[Cu(CN)_4]^{3-}$ is so stable that its dissociation constant is extremely low. When $H_2S$ gas is passed through this solution, the concentration of $Cu^+$ ions is insufficient to exceed the solubility product of $Cu_2S$.
Therefore, no precipitate of metal sulphide is formed.
The amount of $MS$ (or $M_2S$) formed is $0\text{ mol}$.

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are some of the statements about Mn and $\mathrm{Mn}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{7}$. Identify the correct statements. 
 A. Mn forms the oxide $\mathrm{Mn}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{7}$, in which Mn is in its highest oxidation state. 
 B. Oxygen stabilizes the Mn in higher oxidation states by forming multiple bonds with Mn. 
 C. $\mathrm{Mn}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{7}$ is an ionic oxide. 
 D. The structure of $\mathrm{Mn}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{7}$ consists of one bridged oxygen. 
  Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, B and D Only
- **(B)** A, B and C Only
- **(C)** A, B, C and D
- **(D)** A, C and D Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A, B and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Analyzing each statement:

Statement A: In Mn₂O₇, using oxidation state calculation: 2(Mn) + 7(O⁻²) = 0, so Mn oxidation state = +7. This is indeed the highest oxidation state for Mn. TRUE.

Statement B: Oxygen stabilizes Mn in +7 oxidation state through multiple bonding (Mn=O double bonds and bridging), reducing electron density effectively. TRUE.

Statement C: Mn₂O₇ is primarily a covalent oxide with significant Mn-O covalent bonding character. The oxide is not ionic in nature. FALSE.

Statement D: The structure of Mn₂O₇ contains bridged oxygen atoms (Mn-O-Mn linkages) along with terminal Mn=O bonds. TRUE.

Statements A, B, and D are correct while C is incorrect. The answer is option (1): A, B and D Only.

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$\mathrm{MnO}_{4}{ }^{2-}$, in acidic medium, disproportionates to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{MnO}_{4}{ }^{-}$and $\mathrm{MnO}_{2}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Mn}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{7}$ and $\mathrm{MnO}_{2}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Mn}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{7}$ and MnO
- **(D)** $\mathrm{MnO}_{4}{ }^{-}$ and MnO

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{MnO}_{4}{ }^{-}$and $\mathrm{MnO}_{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$MnO_4^{2-}$ (manganate) has Mn in +6 oxidation state.
In acidic medium, it undergoes disproportionation where Mn is simultaneously oxidized and reduced:
Oxidation: $+6 \rightarrow +7$ (forms $MnO_4^-$, permanganate)
Reduction: $+6 \rightarrow +4$ (forms $MnO_2$)
Reaction: $3MnO_4^{2-} + 4H^+ \rightarrow 2MnO_4^- + MnO_2 + 2H_2O$

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The metal ions that have the calculated spin only magnetic moment value of 4.9 B.M. are
A. $\mathrm{Cr}^{2+}$
B. $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$
C. $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$
D. $\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$
E. $\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}$
Choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, C and E only
- **(B)** A, D and E only
- **(C)** B and E only
- **(D)** A, B and E only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A, B and E only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given magnetic moment $=4.9$ B.M.
We know M.M $=\sqrt{\mathrm{n}(\mathrm{n}+2)}$ B.M.
Where, $\mathrm{n} \rightarrow$ No. of unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$
$4.9=\sqrt{\mathrm{n}(\mathrm{n}+2)}$
We get $\mathrm{n}=4$
(A) ${ }_{24} \mathrm{Cr}^{2+} \Rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^4$ (4 unpaired e)
(B) ${ }_{26} \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} \Rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^6$ (4 unpaired e)
(C) ${ }_{26} \mathrm{Fe}^{3+} \Rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^5$ (5 unpaired e)
(D) ${ }_{27} \mathrm{Co}^{2+} \Rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^7$ (3 unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$)
(E) ${ }_{25} \mathrm{Mn}^{3+} \Rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^4 \quad\left(4\right.$ unpaired $\left.\mathrm{e}^{-}\right)$

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The spin only magnetic moment ( $\mu$ ) value (B.M.) of the compound with strongest oxidising power among $\mathrm{Mn}_2 \mathrm{O}_3, \mathrm{TiO}$ and VO is ____ B.M. (Nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Strongest oxidising power among the option is $\mathrm{Mn}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$ because of $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ value
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Mn}^{+3} / \mathrm{Mn}^{+2}}^{\circ}=+1.57 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \mathrm{Mn}^{+3} \rightarrow \mathrm{~d}^4 \text { configuration } \\
& \mu=\sqrt{24} \mathrm{BM} \\
& =4.89 \mathrm{BM} \\
& \Rightarrow 5
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Niobium ( Nb ) and ruthenium $(\mathrm{Ru})$ have " $x$ " and " $y$ " number of electrons in their respective 4 d orbitals. The value of $x+y$ is ______ -.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **11**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Z}=41 \rightarrow \mathrm{Nb} \text { (Niobium) : }[\mathrm{Kr}]_{36} 4 \mathrm{~d}^4 5 \mathrm{~s}^1 \\ & \text { Number of electron in } 4 \mathrm{~d}=4=\mathrm{x} \\ & \mathrm{Z}=44 \rightarrow \mathrm{Ru} \text { (Ruthenium) }[\mathrm{Kr}]_{36} 4 \mathrm{~d}^7 5 \mathrm{~s}^1 \\ & \text { Number of electron in } 4 \mathrm{~d}=7=\mathrm{y} \\ & \mathrm{x}+\mathrm{y}=11\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A transition metal ' \(\mathrm{M}\) ' among \(\mathrm{Sc}, \mathrm{Ti}, \mathrm{V}, \mathrm{Cr}, \mathrm{Mn}\) and \(\mathrm{Fe}\) has the highest second ionisation enthalpy. The spin-only magnetic moment value of \(\mathrm{M}^{+}\)ion is _______. \(\mathrm{BM}\) (Near integer)
(Given atomic number \(\mathrm{Sc}: 21, \mathrm{Ti}: 22, \mathrm{~V}: 23, \mathrm{Cr}: 24, \mathrm{Mn}: 25, \mathrm{Fe}: 26\))

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

Among given metals, $\mathrm{Cr}$ has maximum $\mathrm{IE}_2$
because Second electron is removed from stable configuration $3 d^5$
$\mathrm{Cr}^{+}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^5 4 \mathrm{~s}^0$
$\therefore$ No of unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$in $\mathrm{Cr}^{+}$is $5, \mathrm{n}=5$
$\begin{aligned}
\text { So, Magnetic moment } & =\sqrt{\mathrm{n}(\mathrm{n}+2)} \text { B.M } \\
& =\sqrt{5(5+2)}=5.92 \quad \mathrm{BM} \approx 6
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Give below are two statements:
Statement I : The higher oxidation states are more stable down the group among transition elements unlike p-block elements.
Statement II : Copper can not liberate hydrogen from weak acids.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

On moving down the group in transition elements, stability of higher oxidation state increases, due to increase in effective nuclear charge.
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}^{+2} / \mathrm{Cu}}^{\circ}=0.34 \mathrm{~V} \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{H}^{+} / \mathrm{H}_2}^{\circ}=0 \\ & \mathrm{SRP}: \mathrm{Cu}^{2+}>\mathrm{H}^{+}\end{aligned}$
Cu can't liberate hydrogen gas from weak acid.

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Iron (III) catalyses the reaction between iodide and persulphate ions, in which
A. \(\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}\) oxidises the iodide ion
B. \(\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}\) oxidises the persulphate ion
C. \(\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}\) reduces the iodide ion
D. \(\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}\) reduces the persulphate ion

- **(A)** B only
- **(B)** A only
- **(C)** B and C only
- **(D)** A and D only

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & 2 \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}+2 \mathrm{I}^{-} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+\mathrm{I}_2 \\ & 2 \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+\mathrm{S}_2 \mathrm{O}_8^{2-} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}+2 \mathrm{SO}_4^{2-}\end{aligned}$
$\mathrm{Fe}^{+3}$ oxidises $\mathrm{I}^{-}$to $\mathrm{I}_2$ and convert itself into $\mathrm{Fe}^{+2}$. This $\mathrm{Fe}^{+2}$ reduces $\mathrm{S}_2 \mathrm{O}_8{ }^{2-}$ to $\mathrm{SO}_4{ }^{2-}$ and converts itself into $\mathrm{Fe}^{+3}$.

### Q37. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Arrange the following elements in the increasing order of number of unpaired electrons in it.
(A) \(\mathrm{Sc}\)
(B) \(\mathrm{Cr}\)
(C) \(\mathrm{V}\)
(D) \(\mathrm{Ti}\)
(E) Mn
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \((\mathrm{A})  < (\mathrm{D}) < (\mathrm{C}) < (\mathrm{B}) < (\mathrm{E})\)
- **(B)** \((\mathrm{C})  < (\mathrm{E}) < (\mathrm{B}) < (\mathrm{A}) < (\mathrm{D})\)
- **(C)** \((\mathrm{A})  < (\mathrm{D}) < (\mathrm{C}) < (\mathrm{E}) < (\mathrm{B})\)
- **(D)** \(\text { (B) }  < \text { (C) } < \text { (D) } < \text { (E) } < \text { (A) }\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) \((\mathrm{A})  < (\mathrm{D}) < (\mathrm{C}) < (\mathrm{E}) < (\mathrm{B})\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll} 
\text{Unpaired electron} \\
\operatorname{Sc}[\mathrm{Ar}] 4 \mathrm{~s}^2 3 \mathrm{~d}^1 & 1 \\
\mathrm{Cr}[\mathrm{Ar}] 4 \mathrm{~s}^1 3 \mathrm{~d}^5 & 6 \\
\mathrm{V}[\mathrm{Ar}] 4 \mathrm{~s}^2 3 \mathrm{~d}^3 & 3 \\
\mathrm{Ti}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 4 \mathrm{~s}^2 3 \mathrm{~d}^2 & 2 \\
\mathrm{Mn}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 4 \mathrm{~s}^2 3 \mathrm{~d}^5 & 5
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Among \(\mathrm{CrO}, \mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_3\) and \(\mathrm{CrO}_3\), the sum of spin-only magnetic moment values of basic and amphoteric oxides is _______ \(10^{-2} \mathrm{BM}\) (nearest integer).
(Given atomic number of \(\mathrm{Cr}\) is 24)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **877**

**Detailed Solution:**

CrO Basic oxide
$\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$ Amphoteric oxide
In $\mathrm{CrO}, \mathrm{Cr}$ exist as $\mathrm{Cr}^{+2}$ and have $\mu$ only $=4.90$
In $\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_3, \mathrm{Cr}$ exist as $\mathrm{Cr}^{+3}$ and have $\mu$ only $=3.87$
Sum of spin only magnetic moment
$\begin{aligned}
& =4.90+3.87=8.77 \\
& \mu_{\text {only }}=877 \times 10^{-2}
\end{aligned}$
Ans. 877

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The number of ions from the following that have the ability to liberate hydrogen from a dilute acid is _____.
\(\mathrm{Ti}^{2+}, \mathrm{Cr}^{2+}\) and \(\mathrm{V}^{2+}\)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** 1
- **(D)** 0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

The ions $\mathrm{Ti}^{+2}, \mathrm{~V}^{+2} \mathrm{Cr}^{+2}$ are strong reducing agents and will liberate hydrogen from a dilute acid, eg.
$2 \mathrm{Cr}_{\text {(aq.) }}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{H}_{(\text {aq.) }}^{+} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Cr}_{(\text {aq.) }}^{+3}+\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})$

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The spin-only magnetic moment value of the ion among \(\mathrm{Ti}^{2+}, \mathrm{V}^{2+}, \mathrm{Co}^{3+}\) and \(\mathrm{Cr}^{2+}\), that acts as strong oxidising agent in aqueous solution is _______ BM (Near integer).
(Given atomic numbers : \(\mathrm{Ti}: 22, \mathrm{~V}: 23, \mathrm{Cr}: 24, \mathrm{Co}: 27\))

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Strong oxidising agent $=\mathrm{Co}^{+3}$
No. of unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$in $\mathrm{Co}^{+3}\left[3 \mathrm{~d}^6\right]=4$
Hence $\mu=\sqrt{\mathrm{n}(\mathrm{n}+2)}=\sqrt{24} \mathrm{BM}$
Nearest integer $=5$

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A first row transition metal with highest enthalpy of atomisation, upon reaction with oxygen at high temperature forms oxides of formula \(\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{O}_{\mathrm{n}}\) (where \(\mathrm{n}=3,4\), 5). ### Q45. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

What will be the decreasing order of basic strength of the following conjugate bases? \({ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}, \mathrm{R} \overline{\mathrm{O}}, \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CO} \overline{\mathrm{O}}, \mathrm{C} \overline{\mathrm{I}}\)

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{R} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CO} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>\mathrm{C} \overline{\mathrm{I}}\)
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{C} \overline{\mathrm{l}}>\mathrm{R} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CO} \overline{\mathrm{O}}\)
- **(C)** \({ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{R} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CO} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>\mathrm{C} \overline{1}\)
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{C} \overline{\mathrm{I}}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{R} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CO} \overline{\mathrm{O}}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) \(\mathrm{R} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>{ }^{-} \mathrm{OH}>\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CO} \overline{\mathrm{O}}>\mathrm{C} \overline{\mathrm{I}}\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Strong acid have weak conjugate base Acidic strength :
$\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH}>\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}>\mathrm{R}-\mathrm{OH}$
Conjugate base strength :
$\mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COO}^{-} < \overline{\mathrm{O}} \mathrm{H} < \mathrm{RO}^{-}$

</details>

### Q46. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and the other is labelled as Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> : In aqueous solutions <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is reducing while <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is oxidising in nature.</p><p>Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math> Extra stability to half filled electronic configuration is observed than incompletely filled electronic configuration.</p><p>In the light of the above statement, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="italic">R</mi></mfenced></math> is the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are true but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is not the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is false but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is true
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> is true but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfenced></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="italic">R</mi></mfenced></math> is the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A reducing agent, or reductant, loses electrons and is oxidized in a chemical reaction. A reducing agent is typically in one of its lower possible oxidation states, and is known as the electron donor.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is reducing as it configuration changes from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> due to formation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> which has half filled <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> level, on other hand, the change <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> result half filled <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math>configuration which has extra stability.</p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following compounds show colour due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>·</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CrO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>·</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The hydrated copper sulphate </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is blue in color. The ligand (water molecules) cause splitting of d – orbitals. We know that, due to this, the process of d – d transition and color transition is facilitated.</p><p>Whereas, on the other hand the anhydrous copper sulphate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
 is colourless. In the absence of ligand (water molecules), splitting of d orbitals is not possible. Hence, d – d transition is not possible.
We can understand the above from the following statements.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is blue in colour because its oxidation state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, thus it has unpaired electrons in it. So, d –d transition is possible so it exhibits colour where on the other hand the anhydrous copper sulphate complex, d – d transition is not possible that’s why it is colourless.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p>unpaired electron present so it show colour due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition.</p>

</details>

---

### Q48. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The transition metal having highest <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mi>rd</mi></msup></math> ionisation enthalpy is:

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Ionization energy, in chemistry and physics, the amount of energy required to remove an electron from an isolated atom or molecule. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mi>rd</mi></msup></math> Ionisation energy:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo> </mo><mo>:</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2833</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi><mo>:</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2990</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo> </mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3260</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>2962</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>alternative</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi></math> has highest <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mi>rd</mi></msup><mo> </mo><mi>IE</mi></math> among all the given elements due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math>configuration</p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The 'Spin only’ Magnetic moment for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo> </mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>×</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> BM. (given = Atomic number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo> </mo><mo>:</mo><mo> </mo><mn>28</mn></math>)</p><p>Round off your answer to the nearest integer.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **28**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the presence of ligand <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>, pairing does not occur in   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> because pairing of electrons cannot create two vacant d-orbitals. Hence, nickel ion undergoes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridisation and it has two unpaired electrons.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8bd09ce8-78a6-4812-b416-60c46e8228a5.png" style="width: 250px; height: 60px;"/></p><p>No. of unpaired electron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>The spin only magnetic moment </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>8</mn></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>82</mn><mo> </mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>28</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The orange colour of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub></math> and purple colour of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is due to

**Options:**

- **(A)** Charge transfer transition in both.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> and charge transfer transitions in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub></math>.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub></math> and charge transfer transitions in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition in both.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Charge transfer transition in both.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><ol> <li> <p>The deep blue colour of the solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub></math> is because an electron from the Oxygen lone pair is transferred to low lying Chromate ion orbital.</p> </li> <li> <p>Oxidation number of Chromium in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></math>. The electronic configuration of Chromium is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math>so it will lose six electrons to form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p> </li> <li> <p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>since there are no electrons present in the d-orbital, the d-d transition will not occur.</p> </li> <li> <p>The oxygen atom in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>that act as a ligand and due to charge transfer transfers its one electron to chromium and due to which the coloured compound is formed.</p> </li> <li> <p>Similarly, in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>the oxidation number of Manganese<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></math>. The electronic configuration of Manganese is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math>so it will lose seven electrons to form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></math></p> </li> <li> <p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>, here also no d-orbital is present so no d-d transition will take place.</p> </li> <li> <p>The oxygen atom in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> acts as a ligand and due to charge transfer transfers its one electron to manganese due to which a coloured compound is formed.</p> </li></ol><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> are coloured compounds due to charge transfer or ligand to metal transfer.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Match List-I with List-II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;"> <tbody> <tr> <td> </td> <td>List I (Species)</td> <td> </td> <td>List II (Electronic distribution)</td> </tr> <tr> <td>A.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></td> <td>i.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>B.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math></td> <td>ii.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>C.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></td> <td>iii.</td> <td>.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>D.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math></td> <td>iv.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math></td> </tr> </tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A)-I, (B)-II, (C)-III, (D)-IV
- **(B)** (A)-III, (B) - IV, (C) - I, (D)-II
- **(C)** (A)-IV, (B)-III, (C)-I, (D)-II
- **(D)** (A)-II, (B)-I, (C)-IV, (D)-III

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (A)-III, (B) - IV, (C) - I, (D)-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Electrons must be removed from the outermost orbital to form an ion.</p><p>The electronic configurations of the elements are as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Cr</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>24</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>;</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Mn</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>25</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>;</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Ni</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>28</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>;</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mo> </mo><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>23</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>;</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>→</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math></p><p>Hence option B is the answer.</p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following statements are correct about <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Cd</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> ?
A. They exhibit high enthalpy of atomization as the d-subshell is full.
B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cd</mi></math> do not show variable oxidation state while <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> shows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi>II</mi></math>.
C. Compounds of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Cd</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> are paramagnetic in nature.
D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Cd</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> are called soft metals.
Choose the most appropriate from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** B, D only
- **(B)** B, C only
- **(C)** A, D only
- **(D)** C, D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) B, D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>Cd</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> have low values of enthalpies of atomization. These metals have relatively weak metallic bonding due to their filled or nearly filled d-orbitals. In these metals, the valence electrons are not as delocalized as in other transition metals because the d-orbitals are filled or nearly filled. This leads to weaker metallic bonding, resulting in lower enthalpies compared to transition metals with partially filled d-orbitals. Thus statement (A) is incorrect.
(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cd</mi></math> shows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math> oxidation state whereas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> shows <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math> oxidation state.Hence, statement (B) is correct.
(C) All electrons are paired in the compounds of zinc, cadmium and mercury. Hence, they are diamagnetic in nature. Hence, statement (C) is incorrect.
(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>Cd</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> are soft metals as all electrons are paired and metallic bonds are not strong enough. Thus, statement (D) is correct.</p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Choose the correct option having all the elements with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math> electronic configuration from the following:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Co</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>27</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Ni</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>28</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Fe</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>26</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Cr</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>24</mn></mmultiscripts></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Cu</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>29</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Zn</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>30</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Cd</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>48</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Ag</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>47</mn></mmultiscripts></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Pd</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>46</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Ni</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>28</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Fe</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>26</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Cr</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>24</mn></mmultiscripts></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Ni</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>28</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Cr</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>24</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Fe</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>26</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Cu</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>29</mn></mmultiscripts></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Cu</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>29</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Zn</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>30</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Cd</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>48</mn></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Ag</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mn>47</mn></mmultiscripts></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Electronic Configuration is the distribution of electrons in the various available orbitals of an atom of an element. </p><p>The mentioned elements are present in d-block.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Cr</mi><mo>]</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Cd</mi><mo>]</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Kr</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mo>]</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mo>]</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Kr</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>]</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math></p><p>Hence, the answer is B.</p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2023 (15 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In Chromyl chloride, the oxidation state of chromium is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mo>+</mo></mfenced></math> ___________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The chemical formula of Chromyl chloride is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CrO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. The oxidation state of chromium in chromyl chloride (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CrO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>) is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></math>.</p><p>Let the oxidation number of Chromium be &ldquo;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math>&rdquo;, the oxidation state of oxygen  is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, the oxidation state of chlorine  is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Then,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q55. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Which of the following statements are correct?</p><p>(A) The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> reduction potential for iron is greater than manganese.</p><p>(B) The higher oxidation states of first row d-block elements get stabilized by oxide ion</p><p>(C) Aqueous solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> can liberate hydrogen from dilute acid</p><p>(D) Magnetic moment of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is observed between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math> only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The valence electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math> and that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is stable <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math>.The valence electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math> and that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>The reduction electrode potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> while that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, hence A is wrong. Higher oxidation state of smaller <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math>-block elements is stabilised (or say form compounds) with smaller anion oxide that can be explained by stearic reason hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is correct.</p><p>The oxidation electrode potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> hence, it can reduce <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> and so liberate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>.</p><p>The unpaired electrons in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> hence, the magnetic moment of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> will be lesser than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math>.</p><p>Hence, only <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> are correct.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which halogen is known to cause the reaction given below?

**Options:**

- **(A)** All halogens
- **(B)** Only Bromine
- **(C)** Only Iodine
- **(D)** Only Chlorine

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Only Iodine**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>All <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> halides are known except the iodide. In this case, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> oxidises <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo></msup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>Cu</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>However, many copper (I) compounds are unstable in aqueous solution and undergo disproportionation. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow></mrow></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cu</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>
