### Q89. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In which of the following pairs, the outermost electronic configuration will be the same?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mtext>and&#160;</mtext><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><msup><mi>Co</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mtext>and&#160;</mtext><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Option 1:  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p>Cr is expected to have the highest second ionization enthalpy as after removal of 4s electrons, the electrons will be removed from d-orbital. Cr+ have stable half-filled d-orbital with maximum spin multiplicity. </p><p>Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math></p><p>Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup></math></p><p>Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The incorrect statement among the following is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is an amphoteric oxide
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>VOSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is a reducing agent
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>RuO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is an oxidizing agent
- **(D)** Red colour of ruby is due to the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Red colour of ruby is due to the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>In</mo><msub><mi>VOSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><none/><mo>'</mo><mprescripts/><none/><mo>'</mo></mmultiscripts></math> is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></math> oxidation state.
So it act as oxidising agent.
(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is an amphoteric oxide.
(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>In</mo><msub><mi>RuO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>'</mo><mi>Ru</mi><mo>'</mo></math> is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn></math> oxidation state. So it act as oxidising agent.
(iv) Red colour of ruby is due to the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> ions in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo></math>

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The electrode potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> d-series elements shows positive value for?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Only copper shows positive value for electrode potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo></math>series elements.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>&#920;</mi></msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cu</mi></mrow></mfenced></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The major components in &quot;Gun Metal&quot; are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Sn</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Mg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Ni</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Zn</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Sn</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Gun metal is used for making guns and is also known as red brass. Gun metals are resistant to corrosion that is caused by steam and salt water. That&rsquo;s why their composition includes metals which provides resistance to corrosion. Usually, gun metal is a type of bronze which is an alloy of copper, tin and zinc.</p><p>The major components in &quot;Gun Metal&quot; are</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>87</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sn</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The set that contains atomic numbers of only transition elements, is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 37,42,50,64
- **(B)** 21,25,42,72
- **(C)** 9,17,34,38
- **(D)** 21,32,53,64

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 21,25,42,72**

**Detailed Solution:**

A transition element is defined as the one which has incompletely filled d orbitals in its ground state or in any one of the oxidation state. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>H</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>)</mo></math> are not transition element.

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The INCORRECT statement is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** bronze is an alloy of copper and tin.
- **(B)** cast iron is used to manufacture wrought iron.
- **(C)** german silver is an alloy of zinc, copper and nickel.
- **(D)** brass is an alloy of copper and nickel.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) brass is an alloy of copper and nickel.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left" rowlines="solid" columnlines="solid" frame="solid"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>No</mi><mo>.&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Alloy</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Composition</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Bronze</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>Cu</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Sn</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Brass</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>Cu</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>GermanSilver</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>Cu</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>21</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Cast iron is used to manufacture wrought iron.</p>

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The incorrect statement(s) among (a) - (c) is (are) :</p><p>(a) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>VI</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is more stable than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>VI</mi><mo>)</mo></math>.</p><p>(b) in the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math>, permanganate titrations provide satisfactory results.</p><p>(c) some lanthanoid oxides can be used as phosphors</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (b) and (c) only
- **(B)** (a) and (b) only
- **(C)** (b) only
- **(D)** (a) only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (b) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(a) In transition metals on moving down the group higher oxidation states are more stable due to smaller size of atoms, due to lanthanide and actinide contractions.</p><p>(b) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></math> can oxidise chloride into chlorine, so it will give incorrect results</p><p>(c) its a fact</p>

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Hydrogen has three isotopes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> If the number of neutron(s) in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi>A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>B</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> respectively, are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> the sum of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>3</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Hydrogen has three isotopes
 <table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width:100%;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td>Isotopes</td>			<td>Number of neutrons</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>Protium <math> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow></mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> </msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>Deutrium <math> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow></mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>Tritium  <math> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow></mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>
Hence, the sum of neutrons are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo></math>

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The metal that gives hydrogen gas upon treatment with both the acid as well as the base is

**Options:**

- **(A)** Zinc
- **(B)** Magnesium
- **(C)** Iron
- **(D)** Mercury

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Zinc**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(i) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#8593;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>
(ii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#8593;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>
(iii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math> is amphoteric in nature, so it reacts with acid as well as base.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi>nC</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8593;</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>Zn</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msubsup><mi>ZnO</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8593;</mo></math>
(iv)
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Hg</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>HgO</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the hydrated ions of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> . The correct order of their spin-only magnetic moments is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Magnetic moment <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>  
More than value of n, more will be magnetic moment. 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> electronic configuration; 
<math><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e881cbd5-9687-4f93-b0af-86e2811bf7e5-image39.png"> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2.82</mn></math>  
<math><msup><mrow><mtext>Ti</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/85139260-a4d8-4008-9e46-7b3144a52ff4-image40.png"> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1.73</mn></math>  
<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/aba5ebe6-2763-4960-9f65-72fa92a3f825-image41.png"> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>3.87</mn></math>  
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> no unpaired electron. <math><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  
Hence Order of Magnetic Moment is <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The statement that is INCORRECT about the interstitial compounds is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** They have high melting points.
- **(B)** They are very hard.
- **(C)** They are chemically reactive.
- **(D)** They have metallic conductivity.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) They are chemically reactive.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The principal physical and chemical characteristics of interstitial compounds are as follows:</p><p>(i) They have high melting points, higher than those of pure metals.</p><p>(ii) They are very hard, some borides approach diamond in hardness.</p><p>(iii) They retain metallic conductivity.</p><p>(iv) They are chemically inert.</p>

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The transition elements that has the lowest enthalpy of atomisation is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>C</mi><mi>u</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi></math> is usually not considered as a transition element as the d-orbital electrons do not take part in reactions. This is why <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math> exhibit only <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math>  oxidation state.
But since, it is placed in the d-block along with other transition metals, it is technically a d-block element and a transition metal. Zinc has the lowest enthalpy of atomization among the first row transition metals.</p><table border="1">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td>Elements</td>			<td><math><mi>V</mi></math></td>			<td><math><mi>C</mi><mi>u</mi></math></td>			<td><math><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi></math></td>			<td><math><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>Enthalpy of atomization</td>			<td><math><mn>515</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>J</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mrow></math></td>			<td><math><mn>339</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>J</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mrow></math></td>			<td><math><mn>216</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>J</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mrow></math></td>			<td><math><mn>416</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>J</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mrow></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table></blockquote>

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The<b> incorrect </b>statement is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion gives chocolate coloured precipitate with potassium ferrocyanide solution
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>and</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ions give black precipitate with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> in presence of HCl solution
- **(C)** Ferric ion gives blood red colour with potassium thiocyanate
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> salts give red coloured borax bead test in reducing flame

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>and</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ions give black precipitate with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> in presence of HCl solution**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cu<sup>+2</sup> gives black precipitate in presence of dil. HCl Ni<sup>+2</sup> ions gives black precipitate in presence of NH<sub>4</sub>Cl +NH<sub>3</sub>
with HCl S<sup>2-</sup> concentration is not just sufficient to overcome the solubility product of NiS.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><mi>dil</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HCl</mi></mrow></mover><munder><mrow><msub><mi>Cu</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#8595;</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>black</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></munder><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><munderover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></mrow></munderover><munder><mrow><mi>NiS</mi><mo>&#8595;</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>black</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></munder><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

</details>

### Q105. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 2 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of spin-only magnetic moments among the following is: (Atomic number: $\mathrm{Mn}=$ $25, \mathrm{Co}=27, \mathrm{Ni}=28, \mathrm{Zn}=30$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{ZnCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{ZnCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{ZnCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{ZnCl}_4\right]^{2-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{ZnCl}_4\right]^{2-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The complex having higher number of unpaired electrons will have higher value of spin only magnetic moment.
In all these complexes, the central metal ion is in $+2$ oxidation state.
$\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}$ has $3 d^{10}$ outer electronic configuration with 0 unpaired electron.
$\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}$ has $3 d^8$ outer electronic configuration with 2 unpaired electrons.
$\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$ has $3 d^7$ outer electronic configuration with 3 unpaired electrons.
$\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$ has $3 d^5$ outer electronic configuration with 5 unpaired electrons
Hence the correct order of spin-only magnetic moments is
$$
\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}>\left[\mathrm{ZnCl}_4\right]^{2-} \text {. }
$$

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following are the transition metal ions responsible for colour in ruby and emerald respectively?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Ruby is <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math> in which Red colour is obtained when <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> is replacing <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> ions in octahedral sites. Emerald is <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></math> in which green colour is obtained when <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> is replacing <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> in octahedral site.</p>

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Which one of the following species is stable in aqueous solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>, manganese is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></math> oxidation state, which is having the highest stability.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>MnO</mtext><mn>4</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#8658;&#8203;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math>  It will disproportionate in aqueous solution.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math> It will also disproportionate in aqueous solution.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math> Mn is not stable in +5 oxidation state.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
Higher the oxidation state, more is the stability of the species.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q109. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Find the metallic and ferromagnetic substance.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

In d-block elements,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CrO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math><sub> </sub>exhibit strong attraction to magnetic fields and are able to retain their magnetic properties.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>MnO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is anti-ferromagnetic and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CrO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is ferromagnetic.

</details>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is bluish white.</p>

### Q111. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Copper becomes green when exposed to moist air for a long period. This is due to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** the formation of a layer of cupric oxide on the surface of copper.
- **(B)** the formation of a layer of basic carbonate of copper on the surface of copper.
- **(C)** the formation of a layer of cupric hydroxide on the surface of copper.
- **(D)** the formation of basic copper sulphate layer on the surface of the metal.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) the formation of basic copper sulphate layer on the surface of the metal.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Copper when exposed to moist air having $\mathrm{CO}_2$. It gets superficially coated with a green layer of basic carbonate $\mathrm{CuCO}_3$. $\mathrm{Cu}$ $(\mathrm{OH})_2$.

</details>

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>Li</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>KCl</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>LiCl</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msubsup><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mg</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mi>EDTA</mi></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><mi>excess</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>NaOH</mo></mrow></mover><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mg</mi><mfenced><mi>EDTA</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>KCN</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msubsup><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>Li</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>KCl</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>LiCl</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> </mrow> </math> is incorrect since, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi></math> is less reactive than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>LiCl</mi></math> and will not form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>LiCl</mi></math>.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mg</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mi>EDTA</mi></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><mi>excess</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>NaOH</mo></mrow></mover><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mg</mi><mfenced><mi>EDTA</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is incorrect since, the charge on the complex will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, not <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math>.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CuSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>KCN</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is incorrect since, the final complex formed is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>, not <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>.

</details>

### Q114. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Identify incorrect statement :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Cu}_2 \mathrm{O}$ is colourless.
- **(B)** Copper (I) compounds are colourless except when colour results from charge transfer.
- **(C)** Copper (I) compounds are diamagnetic.
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cu}_2 \mathrm{~S}$ is black.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Cu}_2 \mathrm{O}$ is colourless.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Cu}_2 \mathrm{O}$ is red in colour.

</details>

### Q115. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The element with which of the following outer electron configuration may exhibit the largest number of oxidation states in its compounds :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 d^5 4 s^2$
- **(B)** $3 d^8 4 s^2$
- **(C)** $3 d^7 4 s^2$
- **(D)** $3 d^6 4 s^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $3 d^5 4 s^2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The element with outer electron configuration $3 d^5 4 s^2$ is $\mathrm{Mn}$ which exhibits oxidation states from $+2$ to $+7$.

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><mrow><msup><mtext>Cr</mtext><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mtext>Cr</mtext></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>74</mn><msubsup><mtext>&#160;V;&#160;&#160;E</mtext><mrow><msubsup><mtext>MnO</mtext><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>/</mo><msup><mtext>Mn</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mo>V</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><mrow><msub><mtext>Cr</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mtext>O</mtext><mn>7</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>/</mo><msup><mtext>Cr</mtext><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi><mn>33</mn><msubsup><mtext>&#160;V;&#160;&#160;&#160;E</mtext><mrow><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>|</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>V</mo></math>

Based on the data given above, strongest oxidising agent will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math ><mrow>  <msup>          <mtext>Mn</mtext>      <mrow>        <mn>2</mn>        <mo>+</mo>      </mrow>  </msup></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>MnO</mtext> <mn>4</mn> <mo>-</mo> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow>  <msup>          <mtext>Cl</mtext>        <mo>-</mo>  </msup></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>Cr</mtext> <mrow><mn>3</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>MnO</mtext> <mn>4</mn> <mo>-</mo> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Strongest oxidising agent has the highest tendency to get reduce therefore will have the highest standard reduction potential. So among the given oxidising agents the species have highest value of reduction potential is:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msubsup><mtext>MnO</mtext><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>/</mo><msup><mtext>Mn</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mo>V</mo></math>

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following arrangements does<strong> not</strong> represent the correct order of the property stated against it?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Co<sup>3</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Fe<sup>3</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Cr<sup>3</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Sc<sup>3</sup><sup>+ </sup>:​ Stability in aqueous solution
- **(B)** Sc &lt; Ti &lt; Cr &lt; Mn :​ Number of oxidation states
- **(C)** V<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup>&lt; Cr<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Mn<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> <sup> </sup>&lt; Fe<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup>:​​ Paramagnetic behaviour
- **(D)** Ni<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Co<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Fe<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Mn<sup>2</sup><sup>+ </sup>:​ Ionic size

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) V<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup>&lt; Cr<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> &lt; Mn<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup> <sup> </sup>&lt; Fe<sup>2</sup><sup>+</sup>:​​ Paramagnetic behaviour**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>No. of unpaired electrons 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mtext>V</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mtext>Ar</mtext></mfenced><mn>&#160;4</mn><msup><mtext>S</mtext><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>&#160;3</mn><msup><mtext>d</mtext><mn>3</mn></msup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mtext>Cr</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mtext>Ar</mtext></mfenced><mn>&#160;4</mn><msup><mtext>S</mtext><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>&#160;3</mn><msup><mtext>d</mtext><mn>4</mn></msup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mtext>Mn</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mtext>Ar</mtext></mfenced><mn>&#160;4</mn><msup><mtext>S</mtext><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>&#160;3</mn><msup><mtext>d</mtext><mn>5</mn></msup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mtext>Fe</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><msub><mspace/><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mtext>Ar</mtext></mfenced><mn>&#160;4</mn><msup><mtext>S</mtext><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>&#160;3</mn><msup><mtext>d</mtext><mn>6</mn></msup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>On moving left to right in the periodic table, ionic size decreases.</p><p>Sc shows only one oxidation state, and it is +3 but Mn shows variable oxidation state from +2 to +7.</p>

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The $d$-electron configurations of $\mathrm{Cr}^{2+}, \mathrm{Mn}^{2+}, \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$ and $\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$ are $d^4, d^5, d^6$ and $d^7$ respectively. Which one of the following will exhibit the lowest paramagnetic behaviour?
(Atomic no. $\mathrm{Cr}=24, \mathrm{Mn}=25, \mathrm{Fe}=26, \mathrm{Co}=27$ ).

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Mn}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Fe}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{cc}\text { electronic } & \text { no. of unpaired } \\ \text { configuration } & \text { electrons }\end{array}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8qlCO17NaaL2cx5_1gDRyYqT45Eu0s0WOC7hw5Lpwyo.original.fullsize.png">

$\therefore \quad$ Since $\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$ has lowest no. of unpaired electrons hence lowest paramagnetic behaviour is shown by $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$

</details>

### Q119. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Iron exhibits $+2$ and $+3$ oxidation states. Which of the following statements about iron is incorrect?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Ferrous oxide is more basic in nature than the ferric oxide.
- **(B)** Ferrous compounds are relatively more ionic than the corresponding ferric compounds
- **(C)** Ferrous compounds are less volatile than the corresponding ferric compounds.
- **(D)** Ferrous compounds are more easily hydrolysed than the corresponding ferric compounds.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Ferrous compounds are more easily hydrolysed than the corresponding ferric compounds.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{FeO} \rightarrow$ More basic, more ionic, less volatile

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In context with the transition elements, which of the following statements is incorrect?

**Options:**

- **(A)** In addition to the normal oxidation states, the zero oxidation state is also shown by these elements in complexes.
- **(B)** In the highest oxidation states, the transition metal show basic character and form cationic complexes.
- **(C)** In the highest oxidation states of the first five transition elements (Sc to Mn), all the $4 s$ and $3 d$ electrons are used for bonding.
- **(D)** Once the $d^5$ configuration is exceeded, the tendency to involve all the 3d electrons in bonding decreases.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) In the highest oxidation states, the transition metal show basic character and form cationic complexes.**

**Detailed Solution:**

In higher Oxidation states transition elements show acidic nature

</details>

### Q121. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Identify the incorrect statement among the following

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{d-}$ Block elements show irregular and erratic chemical properties among themselves
- **(B)** $\mathrm{La}$ and $\mathrm{Lu}$ have partially filled $d$ orbitals and no other partially filled orbitals
- **(C)** The chemistry of various lanthanoids is very similar
- **(D)** $4 f$ and $5 f$ orbitals are equally shielded

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $4 f$ and $5 f$ orbitals are equally shielded**

**Detailed Solution:**

$4 \mathrm{f}$ and $5 \mathrm{f}$ belongs to different energy levels, hence the shielding effect is on them is not the same. 
Shielding of $4 \mathrm{f}$ is more than $5 \mathrm{f}$.
Hence (D) is correct.

</details>

### Q125. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following ions has the maximum magnetic moment ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Mn}^{+2}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{+2}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Ti}^{+2}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cr}^{+2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Mn}^{+2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$ has the maximum number of unpaired electrons $(5)$ and therefore has maximum moment.

</details>
